href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup></li><li><a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Venice" title="Patriarch of Venice">Patriarchy of Venice</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup></li><li><a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Bergamo" class="mw-redirect" title="Diocese of Bergamo">Diocese of Bergamo</a><sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup></li><li><a href="/wiki/Sotto_il_Monte" class="mw-redirect" title="Sotto il Monte">Sotto il Monte</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Valsamoggia" title="Valsamoggia">Valsamoggia</a><sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup></li><li><a href="/wiki/Italian_Army" title="Italian Army">Italian Army</a><sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup></li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below"><a href="/wiki/Pope_John_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="Pope John (disambiguation)">Other popes  named John</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Pope John XXIII</b> (<a href="/wiki/Latin_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin language">Latin</a>: <i lang="la">Ioannes XXIII</i>; <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Giovanni XXIII</i>;<sup id="cite_ref-fn1_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fn1-7">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> born <b>Angelo Giuseppe Roncalli</b>, <small>Italian:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="it-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Italian" title="Help:IPA/Italian">[ˈandʒelo dʒuˈzɛppe roŋˈkalli]</a></span>; 25&#160;November 1881&#160;&#8211;&#32;3 June 1963) was head of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> and sovereign of the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City State</a> from 28 October 1958 until his death in June 1963. Angelo Giuseppe Roncalli was one of thirteen children born to Marianna Mazzola and Giovanni Battista Roncalli in a family of <a href="/wiki/Sharecropping" title="Sharecropping">sharecroppers</a> who lived in <a href="/wiki/Sotto_il_Monte_Giovanni_XXIII" title="Sotto il Monte Giovanni XXIII">Sotto il Monte</a>, a village in the province of <a href="/wiki/Bergamo" title="Bergamo">Bergamo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lombardy" title="Lombardy">Lombardy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He was ordained to the <a href="/wiki/Priesthood_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Priesthood (Catholic Church)">priesthood</a> on 10&#160;August 1904 and served in a number of posts, as <a href="/wiki/Nuncio" title="Nuncio">nuncio</a> in <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> and a delegate to <a href="/wiki/Tsardom_of_Bulgaria" title="Tsardom of Bulgaria">Bulgaria</a>, <a href="/wiki/Greece" title="Greece">Greece</a> and <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Nunciature_to_Turkey" title="Apostolic Nunciature to Turkey">Turkey</a>. In a <a href="/wiki/Papal_consistory" title="Papal consistory">consistory</a> on 12 January 1953 <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> made Roncalli a cardinal as the Cardinal-Priest of <a href="/wiki/Santa_Prisca" class="mw-redirect" title="Santa Prisca">Santa Prisca</a> in addition to naming him as the <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Venice" title="Patriarch of Venice">Patriarch of Venice</a>. Roncalli was unexpectedly elected <a href="/wiki/Pope" title="Pope">pope</a> on 28 October 1958 at age 76 after 11 ballots. Pope John XXIII surprised those who expected him to be a caretaker pope by calling the historic <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> (1962–1965), the first session opening on 11&#160;October 1962.
</p><p>John XXIII made many passionate speeches during his pontificate. His views on equality were summed up in his statement, "We were all made in God's image, and thus, we are all Godly alike."<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> He made a major impact on the Catholic Church, opening it up to dramatic unexpected changes promulgated at the Vatican Council and by his own dealings with other churches and nations. In Italian politics, he prohibited bishops from interfering with local elections, and he helped the <a href="/wiki/Christian_Democracy_(Italy)" title="Christian Democracy (Italy)">Christian Democracy</a> party to cooperate with the <a href="/wiki/Italian_Socialist_Party" title="Italian Socialist Party">Italian Socialist Party</a>. In international affairs, his "Ostpolitik" engaged in dialogue with the communist countries of Eastern Europe. He especially reached out to the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox churches</a>. His overall goal was to modernize the Church by emphasizing its <a href="/wiki/Pastoral_role" class="mw-redirect" title="Pastoral role">pastoral role</a>, and its necessary involvement with affairs of state. He dropped the traditional rule of 70 cardinals, increasing the size to 85. He used the opportunity to name the first cardinals from Africa, Japan, and the Philippines. He promoted ecumenical movements in cooperation with other Christian faiths. In doctrinal matters, he was a traditionalist, but he ended the practice of automatically formulating social and political policies on the basis of old theological propositions.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He did not live to see the Vatican Council to completion. His cause for <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonization</a> was opened on 18 November 1965 by his successor, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a>, who declared him a <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>. On 5&#160;July 2013, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> – bypassing the traditionally required second miracle – declared John XXIII a saint, based on his virtuous, model lifestyle, and because of the good which had come from his opening of the Second Vatican Council. He was canonized alongside <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> on 27 April 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> John XXIII today is affectionately known as <b>the Good Pope</b> (<a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">il Papa buono</i>).
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Young_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Young_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg/220px-Young_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="241" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Young_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg/330px-Young_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Young_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg/440px-Young_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg 2x" data-file-width="457" data-file-height="501" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Young_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The young Roncalli</div></div></div>
<p>Angelo Giuseppe Roncalli was born on 25 November 1881 in <a href="/wiki/Sotto_il_Monte" class="mw-redirect" title="Sotto il Monte">Sotto il Monte</a>, a small country village in the <a href="/wiki/Province_of_Bergamo" title="Province of Bergamo">Bergamo</a> province of the <a href="/wiki/Lombardy" title="Lombardy">Lombardy</a> region of <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-fn1_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fn1-7">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> He was the eldest son of Giovanni Battista Roncalli (1854–1935) and his wife Marianna Giulia Mazzola (1855–1939), and fourth in a family of thirteen. His siblings were:<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>Maria Caterina (1877–1883)</li>
<li>Teresa (1879–1954), who married Michele Ghisleni in 1899</li>
<li>Ancilla (1880–1953)</li>
<li>Francesco Saverio (1883–1976), who married Maria Carrara in 1907</li>
<li>Maria Elisa (1884–1955)</li>
<li>Assunta Casilda (1886–1980),<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> who married Giovanni Battista Marchesi in 1907</li>
<li>Domenico Giuseppe (1888–1888)</li>
<li>Alfredo (1889–1972)</li>
<li>Giovanni Francesco (1891–1956), who married Caterina Formenti in 1919</li>
<li>Enrica (1893–1918)</li>
<li>Giuseppe Luigi (1894–1981),<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> who married Ida Biffi in 1922</li>
<li>Luigi (1896–1898)<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>His family worked as <a href="/wiki/Sharecropping" title="Sharecropping">sharecroppers</a>, as did most of the people of Sotto il Monte – a striking contrast to that of his predecessor, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Eugenio Pacelli</a> (Pope Pius XII), who came from an ancient <a href="/wiki/Aristocracy" title="Aristocracy">aristocratic</a> family long connected to the papacy. Roncalli was nonetheless a descendant of an Italian noble family, albeit from a secondary and impoverished branch;<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> "(he) derived from no mean origins but from worthy and respected folk who can be traced right back to the beginning of the fifteenth century". The Roncallis maintained a vineyard and cornfields, and kept cattle.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1889, Roncalli received both his <a href="/wiki/First_Communion" title="First Communion">First Communion</a> and <a href="/wiki/Confirmation_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Confirmation in the Catholic Church">Confirmation</a> at the age of 8.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 1 March 1896, Luigi Isacchi, the spiritual director of his seminary, enrolled him into the <a href="/wiki/Secular_Franciscan_Order" title="Secular Franciscan Order">Secular Franciscan Order</a>. He professed his vows as a member of that order on 23 May 1897.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1904, Roncalli completed his <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_Canon_Law" class="mw-redirect" title="Doctor of Canon Law">doctorate in canon law</a><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> and was <a href="/wiki/Ordination" title="Ordination">ordained</a> a <a href="/wiki/Presbyter" title="Presbyter">priest</a> in the Catholic Church of Santa Maria in Monte Santo in Piazza del Popolo in Rome on 10 August. Shortly after that, while still in Rome, Roncalli was taken to Saint Peter's Basilica to meet Pope Pius X. After this, he would return to his town to celebrate Mass for the Assumption.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Priesthood">Priesthood</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Priesthood">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1905, <a href="/wiki/Giacomo_Radini-Tedeschi" title="Giacomo Radini-Tedeschi">Giacomo Radini-Tedeschi</a>, the new <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Bergamo" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Bergamo">Bishop of Bergamo</a>, appointed Roncalli as his secretary. Roncalli worked for Radini-Tedeschi until the bishop's death on 22&#160;August 1914, two days after the death of Pope Pius X. Radini-Tedeschi's last words to Roncalli were "Angelo, pray for peace". The death of Radini-Tedeschi had a deep effect on Roncalli.
<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> During this period Roncalli was also a lecturer in the diocesan <a href="/wiki/Seminary" title="Seminary">seminary</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bergamo" title="Bergamo">Bergamo</a>.
</p><p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, Roncalli was drafted into the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Italian_Army" title="Royal Italian Army">Royal Italian Army</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Sergeant" title="Sergeant">sergeant</a>, serving in the medical corps as a stretcher-bearer and as a <a href="/wiki/Military_Chaplain" class="mw-redirect" title="Military Chaplain">chaplain</a>. After being discharged from the army in early 1919, he was named <a href="/wiki/Spiritual_direction" title="Spiritual direction">spiritual director</a> of the seminary.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> On 7 May 1921, Roncalli was appointed a Domestic Prelate of His Holiness, which gave him the title of <i><a href="/wiki/Monsignor" title="Monsignor">Monsignor</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> On 6 November, he travelled to Rome where he was scheduled to meet the Pope. After their meeting, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XV" title="Pope Benedict XV">Pope Benedict XV</a> appointed him as the Italian president of the <a href="/wiki/Society_for_the_Propagation_of_the_Faith" title="Society for the Propagation of the Faith">Society for the Propagation of the Faith</a>. Roncalli would recall Benedict XV as being the most sympathetic of the popes he had met.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Episcopate">Episcopate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Episcopate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In February 1925, the Cardinal Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Gasparri" title="Pietro Gasparri">Pietro Gasparri</a> summoned him to the Vatican and informed him of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a>'s decision to appoint him the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_visitor" title="Apostolic visitor">Apostolic Visitor</a> to <a href="/wiki/Tsardom_of_Bulgaria" title="Tsardom of Bulgaria">Bulgaria</a> (1925–1935). On 3 March, Pius XI also appointed him <a href="/wiki/Titular_archbishop" class="mw-redirect" title="Titular archbishop">titular archbishop</a> of <a href="/wiki/Rabba" title="Rabba">Areopolis</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Jordan" title="Jordan">Jordan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Roncalli was initially reluctant about a mission to Bulgaria, but he soon relented. His nomination as apostolic visitor was made official on 19 March.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Roncalli was consecrated a bishop by <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Tacci_Porcelli" title="Giovanni Tacci Porcelli">Giovanni Tacci Porcelli</a> in the church of <a href="/wiki/San_Carlo_al_Corso" title="San Carlo al Corso">San Carlo al Corso</a> in Rome.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On 30 November 1934, he was appointed <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Nunciature_to_Turkey" title="Apostolic Nunciature to Turkey">Apostolic Delegate to Turkey</a> and Greece and titular archbishop of <a href="/wiki/Nesebar" title="Nesebar">Mesembria</a>, Bulgaria.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> He became known in Turkey's predominantly Muslim society as "the <a href="/wiki/Turkophilia" title="Turkophilia">Turcophile</a> Pope".<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Roncalli took up this post in 1935 and used his office to help the Jewish underground in saving thousands of refugees in Europe, leading some to consider him to be a <a href="/wiki/Righteous_Among_the_Nations" title="Righteous Among the Nations">Righteous Gentile</a> (see <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII_and_Judaism" title="Pope John XXIII and Judaism">Pope John XXIII and Judaism</a>). In October 1935, he led Bulgarian pilgrims to Rome and introduced them to <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a> on 14 October.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In February 1939, he received news from his sisters that his mother was dying. On 10 February 1939, Pope Pius XI died. Roncalli was unable to see his mother for the end as the death of a pontiff meant that he would have to stay at his post until the election of a new pontiff. Unfortunately, she died on 20 February 1939, during the nine days of mourning for the late Pius XI. He was sent a letter by Cardinal Eugenio Pacelli, and Roncalli later recalled that it was probably the last letter Pacelli sent until his election as <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> on 2 March 1939. Roncalli expressed happiness that Pacelli was elected, and, on radio, listened to the coronation of the new pontiff.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> In 1939, Roncalli was made head of the Vatican Jewish Agency in Geneva.<sup id="cite_ref-xxiiijews_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-xxiiijews-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Roncalli remained in Bulgaria at the time that World War II commenced, optimistically writing in his journal in April 1939, "I don't believe we will have a war". At the time that the war did in fact commence, he was in Rome, meeting with Pope Pius XII on 5 September 1939. In 1940, Roncalli was asked by the Vatican to devote more of his time to Greece; therefore, he made several visits there in January and May of that year.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> The same year, Roncalli departed as head of the Vatican Jewish Agency and migrated to Turkey.<sup id="cite_ref-xxiiijews_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-xxiiijews-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> However, Roncalli still maintained close relations with the Jews and also intervened to convince Bulgaria's King <a href="/wiki/Boris_III_of_Bulgaria" title="Boris III of Bulgaria">Boris III</a> to cancel deportations of Greek Jews during the <a href="/wiki/Axis_occupation_of_Greece" title="Axis occupation of Greece">Nazi occupation of Greece</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-xxiiijews_37-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-xxiiijews-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Efforts_during_the_Holocaust">Efforts during the Holocaust</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Efforts during the Holocaust">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>As nuncio, Roncalli made various efforts during <a href="/wiki/The_Holocaust" title="The Holocaust">The Holocaust</a> in <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a> to save refugees, mostly Jewish people, from the <a href="/wiki/Nazis" class="mw-redirect" title="Nazis">Nazis</a>. Among his efforts were:
</p>
<ul><li>Delivery of "immigration certificates" to Palestine through the <a href="/wiki/Nunciature" class="mw-redirect" title="Nunciature">Nunciature</a> diplomatic courier.<sup id="cite_ref-Raoul_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Raoul-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Rescue of Jews by means of certificates of "baptism of convenience" sent by Monsignor Roncalli to priests in Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-Raoul_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Raoul-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Children managed to leave <a href="/wiki/Slovak_Republic_(1939%E2%80%931945)" title="Slovak Republic (1939–1945)">Slovakia</a> due to his interventions.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Jewish refugees whose names were included on a list submitted by Rabbi Markus of Istanbul to Nuncio Roncalli.</li>
<li>Jews held at <a href="/wiki/Jasenovac_concentration_camp" title="Jasenovac concentration camp">Jasenovac concentration camp</a>, near <a href="/wiki/Stara_Gradi%C5%A1ka" title="Stara Gradiška">Stara Gradiška</a>, were liberated as a result of his intervention.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Bulgarian Jews who left <a href="/wiki/Tsardom_of_Bulgaria" title="Tsardom of Bulgaria">Bulgaria</a>, a result of his request to <a href="/wiki/Boris_III_of_Bulgaria" title="Boris III of Bulgaria">King Boris III</a> of Bulgaria.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Romania" title="Kingdom of Romania">Romanian</a> Jews from <a href="/wiki/Transnistria" title="Transnistria">Transnistria</a> left Romania as a result of his intervention.<sup id="cite_ref-Raoul_39-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Raoul-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Italian Jews helped by the Vatican as a result of his interventions.<sup id="cite_ref-Raoul_39-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Raoul-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Orphaned children of <a href="/wiki/Transnistria" title="Transnistria">Transnistria</a> on board a refugee ship that weighed anchor from <a href="/wiki/Constan%C8%9Ba" title="Constanța">Constanța</a> to Istanbul, and later arriving in Palestine as a result of his interventions.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Jews held at the <a href="/wiki/Sere%C4%8F" title="Sereď">Sereď</a> concentration camp who were spared from being deported to German death camps as a result of his intervention.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_41-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Hungarian</a> Jews who saved themselves through their conversions to Christianity through the baptismal certificates sent by Nuncio Roncalli to the Hungarian Nuncio, Monsignor Angelo Rota.<sup id="cite_ref-Raoul_39-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Raoul-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>In 1965, the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Herald" title="Catholic Herald">Catholic Herald</a> newspaper quoted Pope John XXIII as saying:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p> We are conscious today that many, many centuries of blindness have cloaked our eyes so that we can no longer see the beauty of Thy chosen people nor recognise in their faces the features of our privileged brethren. We realize that the mark of Cain stands upon our foreheads. Across the centuries our brother Abel has lain in blood which we drew, or shed tears we caused by forgetting Thy love. Forgive us for the curse we falsely attached to their name as Jews. Forgive us for crucifying Thee a second time in their flesh. For we know not what we did.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>On 7 September 2000, the <a href="/wiki/International_Raoul_Wallenberg_Foundation" title="International Raoul Wallenberg Foundation">International Raoul Wallenberg Foundation</a> launched the International Campaign for the Acknowledgement of the humanitarian actions undertaken by Vatican Nuncio Angelo Giuseppe Roncalli for people, most of whom were Jewish, persecuted by the Nazi regime. The launching took place at the Permanent Observation Mission of the Vatican to the United Nations, in the presence of <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Secretary_of_State" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican Secretary of State">Vatican State Secretary</a> Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Sodano" title="Angelo Sodano">Angelo Sodano</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Paus_Johannes_XXIII,_Bestanddeelnr,_254-7064.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Paus_Johannes_XXIII%2C_Bestanddeelnr%2C_254-7064.jpg/220px-Paus_Johannes_XXIII%2C_Bestanddeelnr%2C_254-7064.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="303" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Paus_Johannes_XXIII%2C_Bestanddeelnr%2C_254-7064.jpg/330px-Paus_Johannes_XXIII%2C_Bestanddeelnr%2C_254-7064.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Paus_Johannes_XXIII%2C_Bestanddeelnr%2C_254-7064.jpg/440px-Paus_Johannes_XXIII%2C_Bestanddeelnr%2C_254-7064.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2558" data-file-height="3524" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Paus_Johannes_XXIII,_Bestanddeelnr,_254-7064.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Roncalli as <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Venice" title="Patriarch of Venice">Patriarch of Venice</a></div></div></div>
<p>The International Raoul Wallenberg Foundation has carried out exhaustive historical research related to different events connected with interventions of Nuncio Roncalli in favour of Jewish refugees during the Holocaust. As of September 2000 three reports have been published compiling different studies and materials of historical research about the humanitarian actions carried out by Roncalli when he was nuncio.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2011, the International Raoul Wallenberg Foundation submitted a massive file (the Roncalli Dossier) to Yad Vashem, with a strong petition and recommendation to bestow upon him the title of <a href="/wiki/Righteous_among_the_Nations" class="mw-redirect" title="Righteous among the Nations">Righteous among the Nations</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relations_with_Israel">Relations with Israel</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Relations with Israel">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>After 1944, he played an active role in gaining Catholic Church support for the establishment of the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Israel" class="mw-redirect" title="State of Israel">State of Israel</a>. His support for <a href="/wiki/Zionism" title="Zionism">Zionism</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> and the establishment of Israel was the result of his cultural and religious openness toward other faiths and cultures, and especially concern with the fate of Jews after the war. He was one of the Vatican's most sympathetic diplomats toward Jewish immigration to Palestine, which he saw as a humanitarian issue, and not a matter of biblical theology.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nuncio">Nuncio</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Nuncio">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 22 December 1944, during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> named him to be the new <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Nuncio_to_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Nuncio to France">Apostolic Nuncio to recently liberated France</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> In this capacity he had to negotiate the retirement of <a href="/wiki/Bishop_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop (Catholic Church)">bishops</a> who had collaborated with the <a href="/wiki/German_occupation_of_France_during_World_War_II" class="mw-redirect" title="German occupation of France during World War II">German occupying power</a>.
</p><p>Roncalli was chosen among several other candidates, one of whom was Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Fietta" title="Giuseppe Fietta">Joseph Fietta.</a> Roncalli met with <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Tardini" title="Domenico Tardini">Domenico Tardini</a> to discuss his new appointment, and their conversation suggested that Tardini did not approve of it. One curial prelate referred to Roncalli as an "old fogey" while speaking with a journalist.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Roncalli left Ankara on 27 December 1944 on a series of short-haul flights that took him to several places, such as <a href="/wiki/Beirut" title="Beirut">Beirut</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cairo" title="Cairo">Cairo</a> and Naples. He ventured to Rome on 28 December and met with both Tardini and his friend <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Giovanni Battista Montini</a>. He left for France the next day to commence his newest role.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Cardinal">Cardinal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Cardinal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-More_citations_needed_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">October 2017</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Legion_Honneur_Commandeur_ribbon.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Legion_Honneur_Commandeur_ribbon.svg/170px-Legion_Honneur_Commandeur_ribbon.svg.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="47" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Legion_Honneur_Commandeur_ribbon.svg/255px-Legion_Honneur_Commandeur_ribbon.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Legion_Honneur_Commandeur_ribbon.svg/340px-Legion_Honneur_Commandeur_ribbon.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="218" data-file-height="60" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Legion_Honneur_Commandeur_ribbon.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Legion_of_Honour" title="Legion of Honour">Commander of the Legion of Honour</a> received in 1953</div></div></div>
<p>Roncalli received a message from Mgr. Montini on 14 November 1952 asking him if he would want to become the new Patriarch of Venice in light of the nearing death of <a href="/wiki/Carlo_Agostini" title="Carlo Agostini">Carlo Agostini</a>. Furthermore, Montini said to him via letter on 29 November 1952 that Pius XII had decided to raise him to the cardinalate. Roncalli knew that he would be appointed to lead the patriarchy of Venice due to the death of Agostini, who was to have been raised to the rank of cardinal.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 12 January 1953, he was appointed <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Venice" title="Patriarch of Venice">Patriarch of Venice</a> and raised to the rank of <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">Cardinal-Priest</a> of <a href="/wiki/Santa_Prisca" class="mw-redirect" title="Santa Prisca">Santa Prisca</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a>. Before departing Paris he invited to dinner the eight men who had served as prime minister during Roncalli's term as nuncio.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Roncalli left France for Venice on 23 February 1953 stopping briefly in Milan and then to Rome. On 15 March 1953, he took possession of his new diocese in Venice. As a sign of his esteem, the <a href="/wiki/President_of_France" title="President of France">President of France</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vincent_Auriol" title="Vincent Auriol">Vincent Auriol</a>, claimed the ancient privilege possessed by French monarchs and bestowed the red biretta on Roncalli at a ceremony in the <a href="/wiki/%C3%89lys%C3%A9e_Palace" title="Élysée Palace">Élysée Palace</a>. It was around this time that he, with the aid of Monsignor <a href="/wiki/Bruno_Heim" title="Bruno Heim">Bruno Heim</a>, formed his coat of arms with a lion of Saint Mark on a white ground. Auriol also awarded Roncalli three months later with the award of <a href="/wiki/Legion_of_Honour" title="Legion of Honour">Commander of the Legion of Honour</a>.
</p><p>Roncalli decided to live on the second floor of the residence reserved for the patriarch, choosing not to live in the first floor room once resided in by Giuseppe Melchiorre Sarto, who later became <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a>. On 29 May 1954, the late Pius X was canonized and Roncalli ensured that the late pontiff's patriarchal room was remodelled into a 1903 (the year of the new saint's papal election) look in his honor. With Pius X's few surviving relatives, Roncalli celebrated a Mass in his honor.
</p><p>His sister Ancilla would soon be diagnosed with stomach cancer in the early 1950s. Roncalli's last letter to her was dated on 8 November 1953 where he promised to visit her within the next week. He could not keep that promise, as Ancilla died on 11 November 1953 at the time when he was consecrating a new church in Venice. He attended her funeral back in his hometown. In his will around this time, he mentioned that he wished to be buried in the crypt of Saint Mark's in Venice with some of his predecessors rather than with the family in Sotto il Monte.
</p><p>In 1958, he held a diocesan synod.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Papacy">Papacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Papacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope John XXIII</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:C_o_a_John_XXIII.svg" class="image"><img alt="C o a John XXIII.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/C_o_a_John_XXIII.svg/60px-C_o_a_John_XXIII.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="88" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/C_o_a_John_XXIII.svg/90px-C_o_a_John_XXIII.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/C_o_a_John_XXIII.svg/120px-C_o_a_John_XXIII.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="889" data-file-height="1297" /></a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Holiness</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">Saint</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
