Dante">Enrico Dante</a></th><td class="infobox-data">21 September 1962</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Pietro_Palazzini" title="Pietro Palazzini">Pietro Palazzini</a></th><td class="infobox-data">21 September 1962</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Paul-Pierre_Philippe" title="Paul-Pierre Philippe">Paul-Pierre Philippe</a></th><td class="infobox-data">21 September 1962</td></tr></tbody></table></td></tr></tbody></table> 
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Papal_election">Papal election</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Papal election">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1958_papal_conclave" title="1958 papal conclave">1958 papal conclave</a></div>
<p>Following the death of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> on 9 October 1958, Roncalli watched the live funeral on his last full day in <a href="/wiki/Venice" title="Venice">Venice</a> on 11 October. His journal was specifically concerned with the funeral and the abused state of the late pontiff's corpse. Roncalli left Venice for the conclave in Rome well aware that he was <i>papabile</i>,<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup> and after eleven ballots, was elected to succeed the late Pius XII, so it came as no surprise to him, though he had arrived at the Vatican with a return train ticket to Venice.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Many had considered <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Giovanni Battista Montini</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Milan" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Milan">Archbishop of Milan</a>, a possible candidate, but, although he was the archbishop of one of the most ancient and prominent <a href="/wiki/Episcopal_see" title="Episcopal see">sees</a> in Italy, he had not yet been made a cardinal.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Though his absence from the 1958 conclave did not make him ineligible – under <a href="/wiki/Canon_law_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon law (Catholic Church)">Canon Law</a> any Catholic male who is capable of receiving priestly ordination and episcopal consecration may be elected – the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a> usually chose the new pontiff from among the Cardinals who attend the papal conclave. At the time, as opposed to modern practice, the participating Cardinals did not have to be below age 80 to vote, there were few Eastern-rite Cardinals,<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup> and some Cardinals were just priests at the time of their elevation.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;d&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Roncalli was summoned to the final ballot of the conclave at 4:00&#160;pm. He was elected pope at 4:30&#160;pm with a total of 38 votes. After the long pontificate of Pope Pius XII, the cardinals chose a man who – it was presumed because of his advanced age – would be a short-term or "stop-gap" pope. They wished to choose a candidate who would do little during the new pontificate. Upon his election, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Eug%C3%A8ne_Tisserant" title="Eugène Tisserant">Eugène Tisserant</a> asked him the ritual questions of whether he would accept and if so, what name he would take for himself. Roncalli gave the first of his many surprises when he chose "John" as his <a href="/wiki/Regnal_name" title="Regnal name">regnal name</a>. Roncalli's exact words were "I will be called John". This was the first time in over 500 years that this name had been chosen; previous popes had avoided its use since the time of the <a href="/wiki/Antipope_John_XXIII" title="Antipope John XXIII">Antipope John XXIII</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Western_Schism" title="Western Schism">Western Schism</a> several centuries before.
</p><p>On the choice of his papal name, Pope John XXIII said to the cardinals:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>I choose John... a name sweet to us because it is the name of our father, dear to me because it is the name of the humble parish church where I was baptized, the solemn name of numberless cathedrals scattered throughout the world, including our own basilica [St. John Lateran]. <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_(numbering)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope John (numbering)">Twenty-two Johns</a> of <a href="/wiki/Antipope" title="Antipope">indisputable legitimacy</a> have [been Pope], and almost all had a brief pontificate. We have preferred to hide the smallness of our name behind this magnificent succession of Roman Popes.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Upon his choosing the name, there was some confusion as to whether he would be known as John XXIII or John XXIV; in response, he declared that he was John XXIII, thus affirming the antipapal status of <a href="/wiki/Antipope_John_XXIII" title="Antipope John XXIII">antipope John XXIII</a>.
</p><p>Before this antipope, the most recent popes called John were <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXII" title="Pope John XXII">John XXII</a> (1316–1334) and <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXI" title="Pope John XXI">John XXI</a> (1276–1277). However, there was no <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XX" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope John XX">Pope John XX</a>, owing to confusion caused by medieval historians misreading the <a href="/wiki/Liber_Pontificalis" title="Liber Pontificalis">Liber Pontificalis</a> to refer to another Pope John between <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XIV" title="Pope John XIV">John XIV</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XV" title="Pope John XV">John XV</a>.
</p><p>After his election, he confided in Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Maurice_Feltin" title="Maurice Feltin">Maurice Feltin</a> that he had chosen the name "in memory of France and in the memory of John XXII who continued the history of the papacy in France".<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After he answered the two ritual questions, the traditional <a href="/wiki/Habemus_Papam" class="mw-redirect" title="Habemus Papam">Habemus Papam</a> announcement was delivered by Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Nicola_Canali" title="Nicola Canali">Nicola Canali</a> to the people at 6:08&#160;pm, an exact hour after the white smoke appeared. A short while later, he appeared on the balcony and gave his first <a href="/wiki/Urbi_et_Orbi" title="Urbi et Orbi">Urbi et Orbi</a> blessing to the crowds of the faithful below in <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Square" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Square">Saint Peter's Square</a>. That same night, he appointed <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Tardini" title="Domenico Tardini">Domenico Tardini</a> as his Secretary of State. Of the three cassocks prepared for whoever the new pope was, even the largest was not enough to fit his five-foot-two, 200-plus-pound frame, which had to be let out in certain places and only to be held together with great effort by bobby pins. When he first saw himself in the mirror in his new vestments, he said with an apprising and critical look that "this man will be a disaster on television!", while later saying he felt his first appearance before the globe was as if he were a "newborn babe in swaddling clothes".<sup id="cite_ref-ALL_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ALL-64">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His coronation took place on 4 November 1958, on the feast of Saint <a href="/wiki/Charles_Borromeo" title="Charles Borromeo">Charles Borromeo</a>, and it occurred on the central loggia of the Vatican. He was crowned with the 1877 <a href="/wiki/Palatine_Tiara" class="mw-redirect" title="Palatine Tiara">Palatine Tiara</a>. His coronation ran for the traditional five hours.
</p><p>In John XXIII's first <a href="/wiki/Papal_consistory" title="Papal consistory">consistory</a> on 15 December of that same year, Montini was created a cardinal and would become John XXIII's successor in 1963, taking the name of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Paul VI</a>.  That consistory was notable for being the first to expand the Sacred College membership beyond the then-traditional 70.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Johnxxiii-color-tiara-sm.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9f/Johnxxiii-color-tiara-sm.jpg/220px-Johnxxiii-color-tiara-sm.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="203" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/9/9f/Johnxxiii-color-tiara-sm.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="240" data-file-height="221" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Johnxxiii-color-tiara-sm.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pope John XXIII's coronation on 4 November 1958. He was crowned wearing the 1877 <a href="/wiki/Palatine_Tiara" class="mw-redirect" title="Palatine Tiara">Palatine Tiara</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>Following his election the new pope told the tale of how in his first weeks he was walking when he heard a woman exclaim in a loud voice: "My God, he's so fat!" The new pope casually remarked: "Madame, the holy conclave isn't exactly a beauty contest!"<sup id="cite_ref-ALL_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ALL-64">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Visits_around_Rome">Visits around Rome</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Visits around Rome">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_John_XXIII_-_Porto_Viro_-_Rovigo.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Pope_John_XXIII_-_Porto_Viro_-_Rovigo.jpg/220px-Pope_John_XXIII_-_Porto_Viro_-_Rovigo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Pope_John_XXIII_-_Porto_Viro_-_Rovigo.jpg/330px-Pope_John_XXIII_-_Porto_Viro_-_Rovigo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Pope_John_XXIII_-_Porto_Viro_-_Rovigo.jpg/440px-Pope_John_XXIII_-_Porto_Viro_-_Rovigo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2112" data-file-height="2816" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_John_XXIII_-_Porto_Viro_-_Rovigo.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Monument to Pope John XXIII in <a href="/wiki/Porto_Viro" title="Porto Viro">Porto Viro</a> (<a href="/wiki/Rovigo" title="Rovigo">Rovigo</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>On 25 December 1958, he became the first pope since 1870 to make pastoral visits in his <a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Rome" title="Diocese of Rome">Diocese of Rome</a>, when he visited children infected with <a href="/wiki/Polio" title="Polio">polio</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Bambino_Ges%C3%B9_Hospital" title="Bambino Gesù Hospital">Bambino Gesù Hospital</a> and then visited Santo Spirito Hospital. The following day, he visited Rome's <a href="/wiki/Regina_Coeli_prison" class="mw-redirect" title="Regina Coeli prison">Regina Coeli prison</a>, where he told the inmates: "You could not come to me, so I came to you." These gestures created a sensation, and he wrote in his diary: "... great astonishment in the Roman, Italian and international press. I was hemmed in on all sides: authorities, photographers, prisoners, warders..."<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During these visits, John XXIII put aside the normal papal use of the <a href="/wiki/Majestic_plural" class="mw-redirect" title="Majestic plural">formal "we"</a> when referring to himself, such as when he visited a reformatory school for juvenile delinquents in Rome telling them "I have wanted to come here for some time". The media noticed this and reported that "He talked to the youths in their own language".<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id=".22Ostpolitik.22_and_Eastern_Europe"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="&quot;Ostpolitik&quot;_and_Eastern_Europe">"Ostpolitik" and Eastern Europe</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: &quot;Ostpolitik&quot; and Eastern Europe">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In international affairs, his  "Ostpolitik" ["Eastern policy"] engaged in dialogue with the Communist countries of Eastern Europe. He worked to reconcile the Vatican with the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox Church</a> to settle tensions between the local churches.  The Second Vatican Council did not condemn Communism and did not even mention it, in what some have called a secret agreement between the Holy See and the Soviet Union. In <i><a href="/wiki/Pacem_in_terris" title="Pacem in terris">Pacem in terris</a></i>, John XXIII also sought to prevent nuclear war and tried to improve relations between the Soviet Union and the United States. He began a policy of dialogue with Soviet leaders in order to seek conditions in which Eastern Catholics could find relief from persecution.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relations_with_Jews">Relations with Jews</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Relations with Jews">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII_and_Judaism" title="Pope John XXIII and Judaism">Pope John XXIII and Judaism</a></div>
<p>One of the first acts of Pope John XXIII, in 1960, was to eliminate the description of Jews as <i>perfidius</i> (Latin for "perfidious" or "faithless") in the prayer for the <a href="/wiki/Conversion_of_the_Jews" title="Conversion of the Jews">conversion of the Jews</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Good_Friday_prayer_for_the_Jews" title="Good Friday prayer for the Jews">Good Friday liturgy</a>. He interrupted the first Good Friday liturgy in his pontificate to address this issue when he first heard a celebrant refer to the Jews with that word. He also made a confession for the Church for <a href="/wiki/Anti-semitism" class="mw-redirect" title="Anti-semitism">anti-semitism</a> through the centuries.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While Vatican II was being held, John XXIII tasked Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Augustin_Bea" title="Augustin Bea">Augustin Bea</a> with the creation of several important documents that pertained to reconciliation with Jewish people.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Calling_the_Council">Calling the Council</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Calling the Council">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_John_XXIII_and_Sami_as-Solh.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Pope_John_XXIII_and_Sami_as-Solh.png/220px-Pope_John_XXIII_and_Sami_as-Solh.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="312" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Pope_John_XXIII_and_Sami_as-Solh.png/330px-Pope_John_XXIII_and_Sami_as-Solh.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Pope_John_XXIII_and_Sami_as-Solh.png/440px-Pope_John_XXIII_and_Sami_as-Solh.png 2x" data-file-width="566" data-file-height="802" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_John_XXIII_and_Sami_as-Solh.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>John XXIII with Prime Minister of Lebanon <a href="/wiki/Sami_as-Solh" class="mw-redirect" title="Sami as-Solh">Sami as-Solh</a> in 1959</div></div></div> 
<p>Far from being a mere "stopgap" pope, to great excitement, John XXIII called for an <a href="/wiki/Ecumenical_council" title="Ecumenical council">ecumenical council</a> fewer than ninety years after the <a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">First Vatican Council</a> (Vatican I's predecessor, the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Trent" title="Council of Trent">Council of Trent</a>, had been held in the 16th century). This decision was announced on 25 January 1959 at the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Saint_Paul_Outside_the_Walls" title="Basilica of Saint Paul Outside the Walls">Basilica of Saint Paul Outside the Walls</a>. <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Cardinal Giovanni Battista Montini</a>, who later became Pope Paul VI, remarked to <a href="/wiki/Giulio_Bevilacqua" title="Giulio Bevilacqua">Giulio Bevilacqua</a> that "this holy old boy doesn't realise what a hornet's nest he's stirring up".<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> From the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> came changes that reshaped the face of Catholicism: a <a href="/wiki/Mass_of_Paul_VI" title="Mass of Paul VI">comprehensively revised liturgy</a>, a stronger emphasis on <a href="/wiki/Ecumenism" title="Ecumenism">ecumenism</a>, and a new approach to the world.
</p><p>Prior to the first session of the council, John XXIII visited <a href="/wiki/Assisi" title="Assisi">Assisi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Loreto,_Marche" title="Loreto, Marche">Loreto</a> on 4 October 1962 to pray for the new upcoming council as well as to mark the feast day of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Francis_of_Assisi" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Francis of Assisi">St. Francis of Assisi</a>. He was the first pope to travel outside of Rome since <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX" title="Pope Pius IX">Pope Pius IX</a>. Along the way, there were several halts at Orte, Narni, Terni, <a href="/wiki/Spoleto" title="Spoleto">Spoleto</a>, Foligno, Fabriano, <a href="/wiki/Iesi" title="Iesi">Iesi</a>, Falconara and <a href="/wiki/Ancona" title="Ancona">Ancona</a> where the crowds greeted him.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Moral_theology">Moral theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Moral theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Moral_theology_of_John_XXIII" title="Moral theology of John XXIII">Moral theology of John XXIII</a></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Contraception">Contraception</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Contraception">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Olympische_Spelen_te_Rome,_Paus_Johannes_XXIII_zegent_de_deelnemers_aan_de_Spele,_Bestanddeelnr_911-5390.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Olympische_Spelen_te_Rome%2C_Paus_Johannes_XXIII_zegent_de_deelnemers_aan_de_Spele%2C_Bestanddeelnr_911-5390.jpg/220px-Olympische_Spelen_te_Rome%2C_Paus_Johannes_XXIII_zegent_de_deelnemers_aan_de_Spele%2C_Bestanddeelnr_911-5390.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Olympische_Spelen_te_Rome%2C_Paus_Johannes_XXIII_zegent_de_deelnemers_aan_de_Spele%2C_Bestanddeelnr_911-5390.jpg/330px-Olympische_Spelen_te_Rome%2C_Paus_Johannes_XXIII_zegent_de_deelnemers_aan_de_Spele%2C_Bestanddeelnr_911-5390.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Olympische_Spelen_te_Rome%2C_Paus_Johannes_XXIII_zegent_de_deelnemers_aan_de_Spele%2C_Bestanddeelnr_911-5390.jpg/440px-Olympische_Spelen_te_Rome%2C_Paus_Johannes_XXIII_zegent_de_deelnemers_aan_de_Spele%2C_Bestanddeelnr_911-5390.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2406" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Olympische_Spelen_te_Rome,_Paus_Johannes_XXIII_zegent_de_deelnemers_aan_de_Spele,_Bestanddeelnr_911-5390.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>John XXIII greets sportsmen for the <a href="/wiki/1960_Summer_Olympics" title="1960 Summer Olympics">1960 Summer Olympics</a> on 28 August 1960.</div></div></div>
<p>In 1963, John XXIII established a <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Commission_on_Birth_Control" title="Pontifical Commission on Birth Control">commission</a> of six non-theologians to investigate questions of birth control.<sup id="cite_ref-shannon_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-shannon-71">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-mcclory_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mcclory-72">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Human_rights">Human rights</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Human rights">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>John XXIII was an advocate for human rights which included the unborn and the elderly. He wrote about human rights in his encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Pacem_in_terris" title="Pacem in terris">Pacem in terris</a></i>. He wrote, "Man has the right to live. He has the right to bodily integrity and to the means necessary for the proper development of life, particularly food, clothing, shelter, medical care, rest, and, finally, the necessary social services. In consequence, he has the right to be looked after in the event of ill health; disability stemming from his work; widowhood; old age; enforced unemployment; or whenever through no fault of his own he is deprived of the means of livelihood."<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Divorce">Divorce</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Divorce">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>John XXIII said that human life is transmitted through the family which is founded on the sacrament of marriage and is both one and indissoluble as a union in God, therefore, it is against the teachings of the Church for a married couple to <a href="/wiki/Divorce" title="Divorce">divorce</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Mater_et_magistra,_193_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mater_et_magistra,_193-74">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pope_John_XXIII_and_papal_ceremonial">Pope John XXIII and papal ceremonial</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Pope John XXIII and papal ceremonial">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Ceremonial_of_John_XXIII" title="Ceremonial of John XXIII">Ceremonial of John XXIII</a></div>
<p>Pope John XXIII was the last pope to use full papal ceremony, some of which was abolished after <a href="/wiki/Vatican_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican II">Vatican II</a>, while the rest fell into disuse. His <a href="/wiki/Papal_coronation" title="Papal coronation">papal coronation</a> ran for the traditional five hours (Pope Paul&#160;VI, by contrast, opted for a shorter ceremony, while later popes declined to be crowned). Pope John XXIII, like his predecessor Pius XII, chose to have the coronation itself take place on the balcony of <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">Saint Peter's Basilica</a>, in view of the crowds assembled in <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Square" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Square">Saint Peter's Square</a> below.
</p><p>He wore a number of papal tiaras during his papacy. On the most formal of occasions would he don the 1877 Palatine tiara he received at his coronation, but on other occasions, he used the 1922 tiara of Pope Pius XI, which was used so often that it was associated with him quite strongly. The people of Bergamo gave him an expensive silver <a href="/wiki/Tiara_of_Pope_John_XXIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Tiara of Pope John XXIII">tiara</a>, but he requested that the number of jewels used be halved and that the money be given to the poor.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Liturgical_reform">Liturgical reform</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Liturgical reform">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Maintaining continuity with his predecessors, John XXIII continued the gradual reform of the Roman liturgy, and published changes that resulted in the <a href="/wiki/1962_Roman_Missal" class="mw-redirect" title="1962 Roman Missal">1962 Roman Missal</a>, the last typical edition containing the <a href="/wiki/Tridentine_Mass" title="Tridentine Mass">Tridentine Mass</a> established in 1570 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_V" title="Pope Pius V">Pope Pius V</a> at the request of the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Trent" title="Council of Trent">Council of Trent</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatifications_and_canonization_ceremonies">Beatifications and canonization ceremonies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Beatifications and canonization ceremonies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_saints_canonized_by_Pope_John_XXIII" title="List of saints canonized by Pope John XXIII">List of saints canonized by Pope John XXIII</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_John_XXIII_-_Time_Magazine_Cover_-_January_4,_1963.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Pope_John_XXIII_-_Time_Magazine_Cover_-_January_4%2C_1963.jpg/220px-Pope_John_XXIII_-_Time_Magazine_Cover_-_January_4%2C_1963.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="290" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Pope_John_XXIII_-_Time_Magazine_Cover_-_January_4%2C_1963.jpg/330px-Pope_John_XXIII_-_Time_Magazine_Cover_-_January_4%2C_1963.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/02/Pope_John_XXIII_-_Time_Magazine_Cover_-_January_4%2C_1963.jpg 2x" data-file-width="400" data-file-height="527" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_John_XXIII_-_Time_Magazine_Cover_-_January_4,_1963.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pope John XXIII on <i><a href="/wiki/Time_(magazine)" title="Time (magazine)">Time</a></i> magazine cover of 4 January 1963</div></div></div>
<p>John XXIII beatified four individuals in his reign: <a href="/wiki/Elena_Guerra" title="Elena Guerra">Elena Guerra</a> (26 April 1959), <a href="/wiki/Innocenzo_da_Berzo" title="Innocenzo da Berzo">Innocenzo da Berzo</a> (12 November 1961), <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_Ann_Seton" title="Elizabeth Ann Seton">Elizabeth Ann Seton</a> (17 March 1963) and <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Maria_Palazzolo" title="Luigi Maria Palazzolo">Luigi Maria Palazzolo</a> (19 March 1963).
</p><p>He also canonized a small number of individuals: he canonized <a href="/wiki/Charles_of_Sezze" title="Charles of Sezze">Charles of Sezze</a> and <a href="/wiki/Joaquina_Vedruna_de_Mas" title="Joaquina Vedruna de Mas">Joaquina Vedruna de Mas</a> on 12 April 1959, <a href="/wiki/Gregorio_Barbarigo" title="Gregorio Barbarigo">Gregorio Barbarigo</a> on 26 May 1960, <a href="/wiki/Juan_de_Ribera" title="Juan de Ribera">Juan de Ribera</a> on 12 June 1960, <a href="/wiki/Maria_Bertilla_Boscardin" title="Maria Bertilla Boscardin">Maria Bertilla Boscardin</a> on 11 May 1961, <a href="/wiki/Martin_de_Porres" title="Martin de Porres">Martin de Porres</a> on 6 May 1962, and <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Maria_Pucci" title="Antonio Maria Pucci">Antonio Maria Pucci</a>, <a href="/wiki/Francis_Mary_of_Camporosso" class="mw-redirect" title="Francis Mary of Camporosso">Francis Mary of Camporosso</a> and <a href="/wiki/Peter_Julian_Eymard" title="Peter Julian Eymard">Peter Julian Eymard</a> on 9 December 1962. His final canonization was that of <a href="/wiki/Vincent_Pallotti" title="Vincent Pallotti">Vincent Pallotti</a> on 20 January 1963.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Doctor_of_the_Church">Doctor of the Church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Doctor of the Church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>John XXIII proclaimed <a href="/wiki/Lawrence_of_Brindisi" title="Lawrence of Brindisi">Saint Lawrence of Brindisi</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_the_Church" title="Doctor of the Church">Doctor of the Church</a> on 19 March 1959 and conferred upon him the title "<i>Doctor apostolicus</i>" ("Apostolic Doctor").
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Consistories">Consistories</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Consistories">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_John_XXIII" title="Cardinals created by John XXIII">Cardinals created by John XXIII</a></div>
<p>The pope created 52 cardinals in five consistories, including his successor who would become Pope Paul VI. John XXIII decided to expand the size of the College of Cardinals beyond its limit of seventy that <a href="/wiki/Pope_Sixtus_V" title="Pope Sixtus V">Pope Sixtus V</a> established in 1586.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> The pope also reserved three additional cardinals "<i><a href="/wiki/In_pectore" title="In pectore">in pectore</a></i>" in 1960 which meant he secretly named cardinals without revealing their identities. The pope died before he could reveal these names, therefore meaning that these appointments were never legitimized. John XXIII also sought to further internationalize the College of Cardinals like Pius XII attempted, while also naming the first ever cardinals from countries such as <a href="/wiki/Japan" title="Japan">Japan</a> (<a href="/wiki/Peter_Doi" title="Peter Doi">Peter Doi</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Tanzania" title="Tanzania">Tanzania</a> (<a href="/wiki/Laurean_Rugambwa" title="Laurean Rugambwa">Laurean Rugambwa</a>). Unlike his predecessor, John XXIII held frequent consistories in a marked departure from Pius XII, returning to the frequency seen in the earlier 20th century.
</p><p>John XXIII also issued a rule in 1962 mandating that all cardinals should be bishops; he himself ordained as bishops the twelve non-bishop cardinals in April 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to a June 2007 interview, Loris Francesco Capovilla revealed that <a href="/wiki/Francesco_Lardone" title="Francesco Lardone">Francesco Giuseppe Lardone</a> was one of the cardinals that John XXIII had reserved <i>in pectore</i> in 1960. According to Capovilla, Lardone's precarious position in <a href="/wiki/Turkey" title="Turkey">Turkey</a> meant that he would have to abandon his position if he were named to the cardinalate. Lardone was of the opinion that he could assist bishops in the <a href="/wiki/Iron_Curtain" title="Iron Curtain">Iron Curtain</a> from his posting which he would be unable to do if he was relocated to accept a position in Rome. In November 1960, in preparation for the next consistory, John XXIII offered the cardinalate to Diego Venini who declined the offer.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Vatican_II:_The_first_session">Vatican II: The first session</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Vatican II: The first session">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Giovanni_XXIII_durante_il_Concilio_Vaticano_II_(Lothar_Wolleh).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Giovanni_XXIII_durante_il_Concilio_Vaticano_II_%28Lothar_Wolleh%29.jpg/220px-Giovanni_XXIII_durante_il_Concilio_Vaticano_II_%28Lothar_Wolleh%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="123" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Giovanni_XXIII_durante_il_Concilio_Vaticano_II_%28Lothar_Wolleh%29.jpg/330px-Giovanni_XXIII_durante_il_Concilio_Vaticano_II_%28Lothar_Wolleh%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Giovanni_XXIII_durante_il_Concilio_Vaticano_II_%28Lothar_Wolleh%29.jpg/440px-Giovanni_XXIII_durante_il_Concilio_Vaticano_II_%28Lothar_Wolleh%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2631" data-file-height="1470" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Giovanni_XXIII_durante_il_Concilio_Vaticano_II_(Lothar_Wolleh).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>John XXIII presiding the opening Mass of the Second Vatican Council</div></div></div>
<p>On 11 October 1962, the first session of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> was held in the Vatican. He gave the <a href="/wiki/Gaudet_Mater_Ecclesia" title="Gaudet Mater Ecclesia">Gaudet Mater Ecclesia</a> speech, which served as the opening address for the council. The day consisted of electing members for several council commissions that would work on the issues presented in the council.<sup id="cite_ref-S1-1_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-S1-1-78">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> On the night following the conclusion of the first session, the people in Saint Peter's Square chanted and yelled with the objective of having John XXIII appear at the window to address them.
</p><p>Pope John XXIII appeared at the window and delivered a speech to the people below, and told them to return home and hug their children, telling them that the hug came from the pope. This speech would later become known as the so-called 'Speech of the Moon'.<sup id="cite_ref-Speech2012_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Speech2012-79">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The first session ended in a solemn ceremony on 8 December 1962 with the next session scheduled to occur in 1963 from 12 May to 29 June – this was announced on 12 November 1962. John XXIII's closing speech made subtle references to <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX" title="Pope Pius IX">Pope Pius IX</a>, and he had expressed the desire to see Pius IX beatified and eventually canonized. In his journal in 1959 during a spiritual retreat, John XXIII made this remark: "I always think of Pius IX of holy and glorious memory, and by imitating him in his sacrifices, I would like to be worthy to celebrate his canonization".
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Final_months_and_death">Final months and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Final months and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Peter%27s_Basilica_-_Tomb_of_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/St._Peter%27s_Basilica_-_Tomb_of_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg/220px-St._Peter%27s_Basilica_-_Tomb_of_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/St._Peter%27s_Basilica_-_Tomb_of_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg/330px-St._Peter%27s_Basilica_-_Tomb_of_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/St._Peter%27s_Basilica_-_Tomb_of_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg/440px-St._Peter%27s_Basilica_-_Tomb_of_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1722" data-file-height="1163" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Peter%27s_Basilica_-_Tomb_of_Pope_John_XXIII.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The original tomb of John XXIII (until 2000) in the Vatican necropolis.</div></div></div>
<p>On 23 September 1962, Pope John XXIII was diagnosed with <a href="/wiki/Stomach_cancer" title="Stomach cancer">stomach cancer</a>. The diagnosis, which was kept from the public, followed nearly eight months of occasional stomach hemorrhages, and reduced the pontiff's appearances. Looking pale and drawn during these events, he gave a hint to his ultimate fate in April 1963, when he said to visitors, "That which happens to all men perhaps will happen soon to the Pope who speaks to you today."
</p><p>Pope John XXIII offered to mediate between US President <a href="/wiki/John_F._Kennedy" title="John F. Kennedy">John F. Kennedy</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nikita_Khrushchev" title="Nikita Khrushchev">Nikita Khrushchev</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Cuban_Missile_Crisis" title="Cuban Missile Crisis">Cuban Missile Crisis</a> in October 1962. Both men applauded the pope for his deep commitment to peace. Khrushchev would later send a message via <a href="/wiki/Norman_Cousins" title="Norman Cousins">Norman Cousins</a> and the letter expressed his best wishes for the pontiff's ailing health. John XXIII personally typed and sent a message back to him, thanking him for his letter. Cousins, meanwhile, travelled to <a href="/wiki/New_York_City" title="New York City">New York City</a> and ensured that John would become <a href="/wiki/Time_(magazine)" title="Time (magazine)"><i>Time</i> magazine's</a> '<a href="/wiki/Time_Person_of_the_Year" title="Time Person of the Year">Man of the Year</a>'. John XXIII became the first Pope to receive the title, followed by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">John Paul II</a> in 1994 and <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Francis</a> in 2013.
</p><p>On 10 February 1963, John XXIII officially opened the process of beatification for the late Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Andrea_Carlo_Ferrari" title="Andrea Carlo Ferrari">Andrea Carlo Ferrari</a>, <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Milan" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Milan">Archbishop of Milan</a> from 1894 to 1921. This conferred upon him the title of <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>.
</p><p>On 7 March 1963, the feast of the university's patron Saint <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Aquinas" title="Thomas Aquinas">Thomas Aquinas</a>, Pope John XXIII visited the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_Saint_Thomas_Aquinas" title="Pontifical University of Saint Thomas Aquinas">Pontifical University of Saint Thomas Aquinas, <i>Angelicum</i></a> and with the <i><a href="/wiki/Motu_proprio" title="Motu proprio">motu proprio</a></i> <i>Dominicanus Ordo</i>,<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> raised the <i>Angelicum</i> to the rank of <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Pontifical University">Pontifical University</a>. Thereafter it would be known as the Pontifical University of Saint Thomas Aquinas in the city.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 10 May 1963, John XXIII received the <a href="/wiki/Balzan_Prize" title="Balzan Prize">Balzan Prize</a> in private at the Vatican but deflected achievements of himself to the five popes of his lifetime, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a> to Pius XII. On 11 May, the Italian President <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Segni" title="Antonio Segni">Antonio Segni</a> officially awarded Pope John XXIII with the Balzan Prize for his engagement for peace. While in the car en route to the official ceremony, he suffered great stomach pains but insisted on meeting with Segni to receive the award in the <a href="/wiki/Quirinal_Palace" title="Quirinal Palace">Quirinal Palace</a>, refusing to do so within the Vatican. He stated that it would have been an insult to honour a pontiff on the remains of the crucified <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Saint Peter</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Hebblethwaite_1994_502_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hebblethwaite_1994_502-83">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> It was the pope's last public appearance.
</p><p>On 25 May 1963, the pope suffered another haemorrhage and required several blood transfusions, but the cancer had perforated the stomach wall and <a href="/wiki/Peritonitis" title="Peritonitis">peritonitis</a> soon set in. The doctors conferred in a decision regarding this matter and John XXIII's aide <a href="/wiki/Loris_F._Capovilla" class="mw-redirect" title="Loris F. Capovilla">Loris F. Capovilla</a> broke the news to him saying that the cancer had done its work and nothing could be done for him. Around this time, his remaining siblings arrived to be with him. By 31 May, it had become clear that the cancer had overcome the resistance of John XXIII – it had left him confined to his bed.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>At 11&#160;am <a href="/wiki/Petrus_Canisius_Van_Lierde" class="mw-redirect" title="Petrus Canisius Van Lierde">Petrus Canisius Van Lierde</a> as Papal Sacristan was at the bedside of the dying pope, ready to <a href="/wiki/Anointing_of_the_Sick_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Anointing of the Sick (Catholic Church)">anoint</a> him. The pope began to speak for the last time: "I had the great grace to be born into a Christian family, modest and poor, but with the fear of the Lord. My time on earth is drawing to a close. But Christ lives on and continues his work in the Church. Souls, souls, <i><a href="/wiki/That_they_all_may_be_one" title="That they all may be one">ut omnes unum sint</a>."</i><sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;e&#93;</a></sup>  Van Lierde then anointed his eyes, ears, mouth, hands and feet. Overcome by emotion, Van Lierde forgot the right order of anointing. John XXIII gently helped him before bidding those present a last farewell.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8201;<cite>Peter Hebblethwaite, <i>John XXIII, Pope of the Council</i> (1994), page 502<sup id="cite_ref-Hebblethwaite_1994_502_83-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hebblethwaite_1994_502-83">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup></cite></div></blockquote>
<p>John XXIII died of <a href="/wiki/Peritonitis" title="Peritonitis">peritonitis</a> caused by a perforated stomach at 19:49 local time on 3 June 1963 at the age of 81, ending a historic pontificate of four years and seven months. He died just as a Mass for him finished in Saint Peter's Square below, celebrated by <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Traglia" title="Luigi Traglia">Luigi Traglia</a>. After he died, his brow was ritually tapped to see if he was dead, and those with him in the room said prayers. Then the room was illuminated, thus informing the people of what had happened. The Italian government announced three days of mourning with flags half-masted and the closure of offices and schools.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Francoist_Spain" title="Francoist Spain">Spain</a> announced ten days of mourning with flags half-masted;<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a> announced nine days of mourning with flags half-masted;<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Paraguay" title="Paraguay">Paraguay</a> and <a href="/wiki/Guatemala" title="Guatemala">Guatemala</a> announced three days of mourning;<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_the_Congo" title="Republic of the Congo">Republic of the Congo</a> declared one day of mourning.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> He was buried on 6 June in the Vatican grottos. Two <a href="/wiki/Wreath" title="Wreath">wreaths</a>, placed on the two sides of his tomb, were donated by the prisoners of the Regina Coeli prison and the Mantova jail in Verona. On 22 June 1963, one day after his friend and successor <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a> was elected, the latter prayed at his tomb. John XXIII's tomb is located near the tombs of both <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>.
</p><p>On 3 December 1963, US President <a href="/wiki/Lyndon_B._Johnson" title="Lyndon B. Johnson">Lyndon B. Johnson</a> posthumously awarded him the <a href="/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom" title="Presidential Medal of Freedom">Presidential Medal of Freedom</a>, the United States' highest civilian award, in recognition of the good relationship between Pope John XXIII and the United States of America. The citation for the medal reads:
</p>
<p><i>His Holiness Pope John XXIII, dedicated servant of God. He brought to all citizens of the planet a heightened sense of the dignity of the individual, of the brotherhood of man, and of the common duty to build an environment of peace for all human kind.</i></p><div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatification_and_canonization">Beatification and canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Beatification and canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above n" style="background-color:gold;"><div class="honorific-prefix" style="display:inline;font-size: 77%; font-weight: normal;">Pope <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">Saint</a></div><br /><div style="display:inline;" class="fn">John <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Canonization_of_Pope_John_XXIII_and_Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Canonization of Pope John XXIII and Pope John Paul II">Canonization of Pope John XXIII and Pope John Paul II</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Reliquienschrein_Papst_Johannes_XXIII._-_Petersdom_1.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Reliquienschrein_Papst_Johannes_XXIII._-_Petersdom_1.JPG/220px-Reliquienschrein_Papst_Johannes_XXIII._-_Petersdom_1.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Reliquienschrein_Papst_Johannes_XXIII._-_Petersdom_1.JPG/330px-Reliquienschrein_Papst_Johannes_XXIII._-_Petersdom_1.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Reliquienschrein_Papst_Johannes_XXIII._-_Petersdom_1.JPG/440px-Reliquienschrein_Papst_Johannes_XXIII._-_Petersdom_1.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Reliquienschrein_Papst_Johannes_XXIII._-_Petersdom_1.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The body of John XXIII in the altar of Saint Jerome</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Canonization_2014-_The_Canonization_of_Saint_John_XXIII_and_Saint_John_Paul_II_(14036819834).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Canonization_2014-_The_Canonization_of_Saint_John_XXIII_and_Saint_John_Paul_II_%2814036819834%29.jpg/220px-Canonization_2014-_The_Canonization_of_Saint_John_XXIII_and_Saint_John_Paul_II_%2814036819834%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Canonization_2014-_The_Canonization_of_Saint_John_XXIII_and_Saint_John_Paul_II_%2814036819834%29.jpg/330px-Canonization_2014-_The_Canonization_of_Saint_John_XXIII_and_Saint_John_Paul_II_%2814036819834%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Canonization_2014-_The_Canonization_of_Saint_John_XXIII_and_Saint_John_Paul_II_%2814036819834%29.jpg/440px-Canonization_2014-_The_Canonization_of_Saint_John_XXIII_and_Saint_John_Paul_II_%2814036819834%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="7360" data-file-height="4912" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Canonization_2014-_The_Canonization_of_Saint_John_XXIII_and_Saint_John_Paul_II_(14036819834).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The canonization ceremony of John XXIII and John Paul II</div></div></div>
<p>He was known affectionately as the "Good Pope".<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> His cause for canonization was opened under <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a> during the final session of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> on 18 November 1965,<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> along with the cause of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a>. On 3 September 2000, John XXIII was declared "Blessed" alongside <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX" title="Pope Pius IX">Pope Pius IX</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>, the penultimate step on the road to <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">sainthood</a>, after a miracle of curing an ill woman was discovered. He was the first pope since <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a> to receive this honour. Following his beatification, his body was moved from its original burial place in the grottoes below the <a href="/wiki/St_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="St Peter&#39;s Basilica">Vatican</a> to the altar of <a href="/wiki/St._Jerome" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Jerome">St. Jerome</a> and displayed for the <a href="/wiki/Veneration" title="Veneration">veneration</a> of the faithful.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the time, the body was observed to be extremely well preserved&#160;&#8211;&#32;a condition which the Church ascribes to embalming<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> and the lack of air flow in his sealed triple coffin rather than a <a href="/wiki/Miracle" title="Miracle">miracle</a>. When John XXIII's body was moved in 2001, it was once again treated to prevent deterioration.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> The original vault above the floor was removed and a new one built beneath the ground; it was here that the body of <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> was entombed from 9 April 2005 to April 2011, before being moved for his beatification on 1 May 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 50th anniversary of his death was celebrated on 3 June 2013 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a>, who visited his tomb and prayed there, then addressing the gathered crowd and spoke about the late pope. The people that gathered there at the tomb were from Bergamo, the province where the late pope came from. A month later, on 5&#160;July 2013, Francis approved Pope John XXIII for canonization, along with <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> without the traditional second miracle required. Instead, Francis based this decision on John XXIII's merits for the Second Vatican Council.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> On Sunday, 27 April 2014, John XXIII and Pope John Paul II were declared saints on Divine Mercy Sunday.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The date assigned for the liturgical celebration of John XXIII is not 3 June, the anniversary of his death as would be usual (due to the obligatory memorial of saints <a href="/wiki/Charles_Lwanga" title="Charles Lwanga">Charles Lwanga</a> and his <a href="/wiki/Uganda_Martyrs" title="Uganda Martyrs">companion martyrs</a>), but 11 October, the anniversary of his opening of the Second Vatican Council.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> He is also commemorated in the <a href="/wiki/Anglican_Church_of_Canada" title="Anglican Church of Canada">Anglican Church of Canada</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Evangelical_Lutheran_Church_in_America" title="Evangelical Lutheran Church in America">Evangelical Lutheran Church in America</a>, and some other organizations with a feast day of 3 June or 4 June.<sup id="cite_ref-justus.anglican.org_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-justus.anglican.org-101">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Lutheran_Book_of_Worship_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lutheran_Book_of_Worship-102">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Est%C3%A1tua_do_Papa_Jo%C3%A3o_XXIII_na_Lourinh%C3%A3.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Est%C3%A1tua_do_Papa_Jo%C3%A3o_XXIII_na_Lourinh%C3%A3.jpg/170px-Est%C3%A1tua_do_Papa_Jo%C3%A3o_XXIII_na_Lourinh%C3%A3.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Est%C3%A1tua_do_Papa_Jo%C3%A3o_XXIII_na_Lourinh%C3%A3.jpg/255px-Est%C3%A1tua_do_Papa_Jo%C3%A3o_XXIII_na_Lourinh%C3%A3.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Est%C3%A1tua_do_Papa_Jo%C3%A3o_XXIII_na_Lourinh%C3%A3.jpg/340px-Est%C3%A1tua_do_Papa_Jo%C3%A3o_XXIII_na_Lourinh%C3%A3.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="800" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Est%C3%A1tua_do_Papa_Jo%C3%A3o_XXIII_na_Lourinh%C3%A3.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Statue of John XXIII in Portugal</div></div></div>
<p>From his teens when he entered the seminary, he maintained a diary of spiritual reflections that was subsequently published as the <i>Journal of a Soul</i>. The collection of writings charts Roncalli's goals and his efforts as a young man to "grow in holiness" and continues after his election to the papacy; it remains widely read.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The opening titles of <a href="/wiki/Pier_Paolo_Pasolini" title="Pier Paolo Pasolini">Pier Paolo Pasolini</a>'s film <i><a href="/wiki/The_Gospel_According_to_St._Matthew_(film)" title="The Gospel According to St. Matthew (film)">The Gospel According to St. Matthew</a></i> (1964) dedicate the film to the memory of John XXIII.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/John_XXIII_College_(Perth)" class="mw-redirect" title="John XXIII College (Perth)">John XXIII College (Perth)</a> in <a href="/wiki/Western_Australia" title="Western Australia">Western Australia</a> is a Catholic school named after John XXIII, Pope John Senior High School and Junior Seminary in Koforidua, Ghana and the Catholic Learning Community of John XXIII, a primary school in <a href="/wiki/Sydney" title="Sydney">Sydney</a>. <a href="/wiki/Roncalli_College" title="Roncalli College">Roncalli College</a> is located in <a href="/wiki/Timaru" title="Timaru">Timaru</a>, New Zealand. There are also Roncalli High Schools in <a href="/wiki/Roncalli_High_School_(Indiana)" title="Roncalli High School (Indiana)">Indianapolis, Indiana</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roncalli_High_School_(Aberdeen,_South_Dakota)" class="mw-redirect" title="Roncalli High School (Aberdeen, South Dakota)">Aberdeen, South Dakota</a>, <a href="/wiki/Manitowoc,_Wisconsin" title="Manitowoc, Wisconsin">Manitowoc, Wisconsin</a>, and Omaha, Nebraska. There is also John XXIII School in <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a><sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During an event held on 6 May 2019 in Bulgaria, Pope Francis invoked John XXIII's encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Pacem_in_terris" title="Pacem in terris">Pacem in terris</a></i> as a "code of conduct" for peace between Catholics and other religions.<sup id="cite_ref-bulgariaprotect_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bulgariaprotect-108">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Sacred_Heart_University" title="Sacred Heart University">Sacred Heart University</a> in <a href="/wiki/Fairfield,_Connecticut" title="Fairfield, Connecticut">Fairfield, Connecticut</a>, has a residence hall named after him, called Angelo Roncalli Hall.<sup id="cite_ref-Angelo_Roncalli_Hall_2021_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Angelo_Roncalli_Hall_2021-110">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_XXIII&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-fn1-7"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-fn1_7-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-fn1_7-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text">In his native <a href="/wiki/Lombard_language" title="Lombard language">Lombard language</a>, his <a href="/wiki/Papal_name" title="Papal name">papal name</a> is rendered <i><b>Giuànn XXIII</b></i>; his birth name as <i><b>Angel Giüsepp Roncalli</b></i>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-57"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-57">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">William Doino is one of the commentators who claim that Roncalli was <i>papabile</i> and argue that "[b]y the time of Pius XII’s death, in 1958, Cardinal Roncalli 'contrary to the idea he came out of nowhere to become pope' was actually one of those favored to be elected. He was well known, well liked and trusted."<sup id="cite_ref-FirstThings_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FirstThings-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-60"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-60">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">At the 1958 conclave, the two Eastern Catholic cardinal-electors were <a href="/wiki/Gregorio_Pietro_Agagianian" title="Gregorio Pietro Agagianian">Gregorio Pietro Agagianian</a>, Patriarch of Cilicia of the Armenian Catholic Church and <a href="/wiki/Ignatius_Gabriel_I_Tappouni" title="Ignatius Gabriel I Tappouni">Ignatius Gabriel I Tappouni</a>, Patriarch of Antioch of the Syrian Catholic Church</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-61"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-61">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">At the 1958 conclave, <a href="/wiki/Nicola_Canali" title="Nicola Canali">Nicola Canali</a> the Cardinal protodeacon was only an ordained priest and <a href="/wiki/Alfredo_Ottaviani" title="Alfredo Ottaviani">Alfredo Ottaviani</a>, the Cardinal-Deacon of <i>Santa Maria in Domnica</i> had not yet been consecrated as a bishop.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-84"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-84">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"> ‘...that all may be one.'</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
